```
def project_box_sum(v, n, target):
    h=np.clip(v,0,1)
    for it in range(100):
      s=h.sum(); diff=target-s
      if abs(diff)<1e-12: break
      if diff>0:
        room=1-h; tot=room.sum()
        if tot<1e-15: break
        # add diff to variables sorted room? simple proportional ok may overshoot? h+=diff*room/tot then clip again
      else:
        tot=s
        if tot<1e-15: break
        # Need reduce. If proportional h *= (target/s) may drop below 0 if some tiny? but s>=target? h_all positive? target up to n/2. proportional safe if all nonnegative and target<=s; some may become 0 but no negative.
        h *= target/s
    return np.clip(h,0,1)
```
Proportional increase with room ensures no overshoot? h_i + diff*room_i/tot ≤1 because diff≤tot? Need diff≤tot yes target≤n, current sum≥0. It reaches at most 1. Good. Decrease proportional to h ensures no negative. Converges exact? After add proportional then clip none. For decrease, h *= target/s exactly to target? Sum becomes target if all clipped none. Since target≤s and h_i scale, sum scale target. No clip needed if target≥0. So one step. But if some h_i =1 and diff>0, room zero fine. For diff<0, h scale; if sum s>0. So simpler. But if diff>0 and target-s > tot? cannot since target≤n. Use one loop.
Need if s=0 and diff>0; add evenly to room? room=1 all, tot=n, h=diff/n <=1 if target≤n. Good.
So:
```
if diff>0:

else:

```
def compute_c_fft(h, n, L):
    H = np.fft.rfft(h, n=L)
    r = np.fft.irfft(H * np.conj(H), n=L)  # multiply complex; maybe np.fft.irfft(H * np.conj(H), n=L)
    # r[t] = ∑ h_i h_{i+t} for t=0..n-1? Need verify with conj.

For t positive, zero padding gives desired for t<n. Good. So r[0]=sum h^2; r[d]=∑ h_i h_{i+d} for i=0..n-d-1. For negative offsets, r[L-d]=∑ h_i h_{i-d}=A_d.
    auto_pos = r[:n-1]  # A_d for d=1..n-1
    prefix = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(h))) length n+1
    V_pos = prefix[n - np.arange(n)] - np.concatenate(([sum(h - h*h?)? )?])

```
pos = np.empty(n)

d=np.arange(n)

```
For negative offsets order in full array: offset -d for d=n-1..1: value V_-d = ∑_{i=d}^{n-1} h_i - A_d = prefix[n]-prefix[d] - A_d. Full c = [V_{-(n-1)},...,V_{-1}, V_0, V_1,...,V_{n-1}]. So:
```

def softmax_weights(c, alpha):

```
def exact_subgrad(h,n,L,deadline,max_iter,step_init=0.02):
   best=...
   step=step_init
   stall=0
   for it...:
      vmax, cvals = compute_c_max...
      active = top based on cvals >= vmax - max(1e-8,1e-4*vmax)
      w = top M uniform
      g = gradient_weights(h,n,L,w)
      g -= g.mean()
      gnorm = np.linalg.norm(g)
      if gnorm < 1e-12: break
      d = -g/gnorm
      # if direction conflicts with bounds? projected handles
      improved=False
      for trial in range(20):
         h_new=np.clip(h+step*d,0,1); project
         newv=compute_c_max_v(h_new,n,L)
         if newv < best_v - 1e-12: accept; improved=True; break
         step *= 0.7
         if step < 1e-10: break
      if not improved:
         step *=0.5; stall+=1; if stall>50 break
   return best
```

        # Try moving to some j high with h[j]>=step

Implement prefix and auto carefully:
```
def compute_c_full(h,n,L):
    H = np.fft.rfft(h, n=L)
    r = np.fft.irfft(H * np.conjugate(H), n=L)
    # maybe small negative due round; auto = r[:n]
    prefix = np.empty(n+1); prefix[0]=0; prefix[1:]=np.cumsum(h)
    c = np.empty(2*n-1, dtype=float)
    total = prefix[n]
    sq = float(np.dot(h,h))
    # negative: d = n-1, ...,1
    d_vec = np.arange(n-1,0,-1)  # length n-1
    c[:n-1] = total - prefix[d_vec] - r[d_vec]
    c[n-1] = total - sq
    # positive d=0..n-1
    # low sums: prefix[n-d]
    d_pos = np.arange(n)
    # auto_pos: shift0 sq, r[1:n]
    c[n-1:] = prefix[n-d_pos] - np.concatenate(([sq], r[1:n]))
    return c
```
Need check r[d] for d up to n-1 valid. L≥2n-1, yes. For d=n? no.
Could small negative values due FFT; c maybe slightly. Max okay.

`compute_c_max(h,n,L)` can compute same and max. To avoid allocate full twice, in smooth value need c to compute logsum; local exact maybe only max. For value, allocate c. For grad, need c and weights, allocate c. For exact search, max only: can compute auto and prefix and max without full array? We can optimize:
- Need max over d≥0 pos: max(prefix[n-d] - A_d) and d≥1 neg: total-prefix[d]-A_d. Could compute arrays without full c. But c allocation O(n) trivial. For exact search many calls, full array maybe okay. We can implement `compute_c_max_val(h,n,L)` returning maxV without full c? Let's do to save memory? Maybe not needed. But local search thousands; allocate c length 2n (16k) each time okay. To be faster, implement direct max:
```
def compute_c_max_val(h,n,L):
  H=rfft; r=irfft; prefix; total; sq;
  maxv=total-sq
  if n>1:
    d=np.arange(1,n)
    A=r[1:n]
    pos = prefix[n-d] - A
    neg = total - prefix[d] - A
    maxv = max(maxv, pos.max(), neg.max())
  return float(maxv)
```
No c array. For active gradient, need c to select active. Use full.

Direct gradient from weights by shifts:
```
def gradient_direct(h,n,w):
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    nonzero = np.nonzero(w)[0]
    center=n-1
    dpos? For each idx in nonzero:
      sig = float(w[idx])
      k = idx - center
      if k==0: grad += sig*(1-2*h)
      elif k>0:
        nv=n-k
        grad[:nv] += sig*(1-h[k:])
        grad[k:] -= sig*h[:nv]
      else:
        kk=-k; nv=n-kk
        grad[kk:] += sig*(1-h[:nv])
        grad[:nv] -= sig*h[kk:]
    return grad
```
This matches V offsets. For w sum 1, entries O(1). Use if nonzero count <= maybe 128 and n small? For n=8192, 128*8192=1M vector ops, maybe slower than FFT (100k). But Python loop 128 with slice operations each O(n) could be ~128*1M? Actually each slice length ~n, 128*8192 ~1M, but Python loop overhead 128; okay maybe 0.005s. FFT K also 0.002. Use threshold 32. For local top active maybe 20; direct okay. For smooth weights dense -> FFT. We can write `gradient_from_weights(h,n,w,L=None)` that if sparse and L not None maybe choose direct vs FFT based on count*n < L*logL*constant. Simpler: in smooth grad use `gradient_fft`; in local use direct if top count small, else fft.

Need ensure w array exact sum not need 1? Gradient of weighted sum. For softmax weights sum 1. In direct, if weights underflow near zero, nonzero includes tiny; use threshold 1e-14.

`softmax_weights(c, alpha)` for C scale:
```
def softmax_weights(c, alpha):
    m=c.max()
    z=alpha*(c-m)
    # max z=0
    w=np.exp(z)  # overflow no; underflow okay
    s=w.sum()
    if s<=0:
      idx=np.argmax(c); w[idx]=1.0
    else: w/=s
    return w
```
If alpha=0? We won't.

`smooth_obj(x,n,L,alpha,mu)`:
```
def smooth_obj(x,n,L,alpha,mu):
    c = compute_c_full(x,n,L) * (2.0/n)
    m = c.max()
    z=alpha*(c-m)
    # max=0
    val = m + np.log(np.sum(np.exp(z)))/alpha
    e = x.sum() - n/2.0
    if mu != 0: val += 0.5*mu*e*e
    return float(val)
```
Potential sum exp underflow if alpha large and z very negative for many; @ least one z=0 exp=1 so sum>=1. Good.

`smooth_grad`:
```
def smooth_grad(x,n,L,alpha,mu):
   c=compute_c_full(x,n,L)*(2.0/n)
   w=softmax_weights(c,alpha)
   dx=2.0/n
   # choose gradient FFT or direct based on nonzero
   nonzero=np.count_nonzero(w > 1e-14)
   if nonzero <= 32:
       grad=gradient_direct(x,n,w)
   else:
       grad=gradient_fft(x,n,L,w)
   grad *= dx
   e = x.sum() - n/2.0
   if mu !=0: grad += mu*e
   return grad
```
If using no projection, x may slightly outside [0,1]? L-BFGS bounds ensures within. But if x has NaN? no.

Define `gradient_fft`:
```
def gradient_fft(h,n,L,w):
   center=n-1
   pos=w[center:]
   neg=w[:center][::-1]? Wait earlier neg_abs[1:] = w[:center][::-1]. We'll handle.
   v=np.zeros(n)
   v[0]=2*pos[0]
   if n>1: v[1:]=pos[1:]+w[:center][::-1]
   # linear
   lin=np.full(n, pos[0])
   if n>1:
     p=pos[1:]
     cum=np.cumsum(p)
     totalp=cum[-1]
     before=np.empty(n-1); before[0]=0; before[1:]=cum[:-1]
     lin[:-1] += totalp - before
     neg_vals=w[:center][::-1]
     cumn=np.cumsum(neg_vals)
     lin[1:] += cumn
   K=np.zeros(L)
   K[0]=v[0]
   if n>1:
     K[1:n]=v[1:]
     K[L-(n-1):]=v[1:][::-1]
   H=np.fft.rfft(h,n=L)
   KH=np.fft.rfft(K,n=L)
   conv=np.fft.irfft(H*KH,n=L)[:n]
   return lin - conv
```
Check `lin` for negative: if w offset -d, value V[-d]=∑_{i=d}^{n-1} h_i - A_d. Linear at i if i≥d, coefficient w_-d. For j, sum_{d≤j} w_-d. `neg_vals[d-1]` = w offset -d (d=1 at index center-1? w[:center][::-1]: w[center-1] offset -1 first -> d=1. Good). cumn[j-1] for j≥1. Good.

Potential K with L-(n-1) assignment: if n-1=0? handle. For L power of two, L-(n-1) > n? yes since L≥2n-1 >2(n-1), so no overlap with K[1:n] except if 2n-1? L=2n-1 if power? L is power >=2n-1; if L=2n-1 (e.g., n? 2n-1 power of 2 possible n=3 ->5 not power, L=8; okay). K nonzero shifts at both ends no overlap for d≤n-1 because L-(n-1) >= n? For L=8,n=4, L-3=5, K[1:4] and K[5:8] distinct.

Now exact active gradient via direct from top weights. We can create weight vector or list of (idx, weight) to avoid dense. Helper `gradient_from_pairs(h,n,pairs)`, pairs list of (offset_idx, weight). Use direct. For local, we can select active idx list and weights uniform/softmax.

Function `top_active_weights(c, max_m, alpha=None)`:
- c full C or V (scale irrelevant). vmax=c.max().
- If alpha is None: active = where(c >= vmax - tol*(1+abs(vmax))) maybe uniform.
- If len > max_m: sort top.
- weights: if alpha positive: w = softmax over top? Could dense. But for gradient direct, only top. Use `w_vals = exp(alpha*(c[top]-vmax))` normalized (sum may underflow). If alpha None: ones/len.
Return list of (idx, val/m). If using softmax over top only, ignores lower; okay approximates. Or just uniform top for subgradient.

Pseudo:
```
def active_average_gradient(h,n,L, max_m=80, tol=1e-7, alpha=None):
   c=compute_c_full(h,n,L) * dx? (scale irrelevant) maybe V
   vmax=c.max()
   tol = max(1e-10, 1e-6*vmax)  # V scale vmax ~ n*0.19? absolute tol maybe? Use relative 1e-7?
   active=np.where(c >= vmax - tol)[0]
   if len(active)==0: active=np.array([n-1])
   if len(active)>max_m:
       top = active[np.argsort(-c[active])[:max_m]]
   else: top=active
   w = np.ones(len(top))/len(top)
   # optional softmax emphasis: if len(top)<active_count? not
   grad = gradient_from_indices(h,n,top,w)
   grad -= grad.mean()
   return grad, vmax, c
```
Direct gradient from indices:
```
def gradient_from_indices(h,n, idx, vals):
  grad=np.zeros(n)
  center=n-1
  for idx_i,val in zip(idx,vals):
    k=idx_i-center
    if k==0: grad += val*(1-2*h)
    elif k>0: ...
  return grad
```

Subgrad line search:
```
def subgradient_step(h,n,L,deadline, step=0.01, max_iter= ...):
  best_h=h.copy(); best_v=compute_c_max_val(...)
  step_cur=step
  no_improve=0
  for it in range(max_iter):
    if time.time()>deadline: break
    g,vmax,c = active_average_gradient(...)
    if vmax < best_v: best_v=vmax; best_h=h.copy()
    g -= g.mean()
    gn = np.linalg.norm(g)
    if gn < 1e-12: break
    d = -g/gn  # normalized
    # optional if direction mostly violates bounds, projection will clip
    accepted=False
    for trial in range(30):
        h_new = h + step_cur*d
        h_new = np.clip(h_new,0,1)
        h_new = project_feasible(h_new,n)
        vnew=compute_c_max_val(h_new,n,L)
        if vnew < best_v - 1e-13:
            h=h_new; best_v=vnew; best_h=h.copy(); improved=True; accepted=True; break
        step_cur *= 0.7
        if step_cur < 1e-12: break
    if not accepted:
        no_improve +=1; step_cur *=0.5
        if no_improve>80: break
        # if no improve, maybe try a random perturbation? no
    else:
       no_improve=0
       # maybe keep step or reduce slightly
       step_cur = min(step_cur*1.1, 0.1)
  return best_h,best_v
```

Actually d normalized to norm1 gives each component ~1/sqrt(n)=0.01 for n=8192? Norm of vector of ones/sqrt(n)=1. Step 0.01 moves 1e-4. Too small to improve significantly. Better set d = -g without normalization, and step scale relative to g magnitude? If g entries O(1), norm O(sqrt(n)) ~90. d_unnorm changes entry O(step). We can choose step based on Lipschitz? def pair_move_optimize(h,n,L,deadline, top_count=100, step_init=0.02):
  best_v=...
  for step in [step_init, step_init/2, ... 1e-6]:
    improved=True
    max_sweeps=???
    while improved and time<deadline:
      improved=False
      c=compute_c_full...
      active top etc
      g=direct gradient active uniform/softmax
      g_abs = max(abs(g),1e-12)
      low = np.argsort(g)[:top_count]
      high = np.argsort(g)[::-1][:top_count]
      for i in low:
        if h[i] + step >1: continue
        # choose up to e.g. 20 candidates from high with h[j]>=step
        for j in high_candidates:
           if i==j or h[j]<step: continue
           h[i]+=step; h[j]-=step
           v=compute_c_max_val
           if v<best_v-1e-12:
              best_h=h.copy(); best_v=v; improved=True; break
           else: revert
        if improved: break
      if improved: continue outer? recompute gradient
  return best_h,best_v
```

```
def force_sum_exact(h,n):
  target=n/2.0
  for _ in range(5):
    s=float(np.sum(h, dtype=np.float64))
    diff=target-s
    if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
    if diff>0:
       idx=int(np.argmax(1.0-h))
       if 1.0-h[idx] < diff: diff=1.0-h[idx]
       h[idx]+=diff
    else:
       idx=int(np.argmax(h))
       if h[idx] < -diff: diff=-h[idx]
       h[idx]+=diff
  return np.clip(h,0,1)
```
Sum still approximate but fine.

```
def run_single_lbfgs(h,n,L,alpha,mu,maxiter=500,ftol=1e-15,gtol=1e-12):
  bounds=[(0,1)]*n
  try:
    res=minimize(smooth_obj, h, jac=smooth_grad, args=(n,L,alpha,mu), method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxiter*10,'ftol':ftol,'gtol':gtol,'maxls':40})
    h_new=project_feasible(res.x,n)
  except: h_new=h
  return project_feasible(h_new,n)
```

def pair_move_search(h,n,L,deadline, top_count=128, step_init=0.04, min_step=1e-6, max_noimprove=3):
    best_h=h.copy(); best_v=compute_c_max_val(...)
    step=step_init
    for trial step in [0.04,0.02,..., min]:
       no_sweeps=0
       while time<deadline and no_sweeps<3:
          c=compute_c_full(h,n,L)
          active weights maybe softmax over top 2*top_count
          g=gradient_from_indices or fft
          low = np.argsort(g)[:top_count]
          high = np.argsort(g)[::-1][:top_count]
          improved=False
          any_trial=False
          for i in low:
             if h[i] < step? Not; increasing i requires h_i+step≤1.
             # select up to 20 j from high
             for j in high_up_to_30:
                if j==i or h[j]<step: continue
                h[i]+=step; h[j]-=step
                v=compute_c_max_val(h,n,L)
                if v < best_v - 1e-13:
                    best_h=h.copy(); best_v=v; improved=True
                    any_trial=True
                    break
                else: h[i]-=step; h[j]+=step
             if improved: break
          if not improved:
             no_sweeps +=1
          if time... break
       step/=2
    return best_h
```

def lbfgs_pipeline(h,n,L,deadline, stages, reserve=5):
   bounds=[(0,1)]*n
   best_h=h.copy(); best_v=compute_c_max_val(h,n,L)
   for alpha,mu,maxiter in stages:
       if time.time() > deadline - reserve: break
       # estimate remaining time? set maxiter based on n:
       # We can't know time per iter; use fixed maxiter but check before start.
       h_prev=best_h
       try res...
       h_new=project_feasible(res.x,n)
       v=compute_c_max_val(h_new,n,L)
       if v < best_v - 1e-15:
           best_v=v; best_h=h_new.copy()
       # else keep best_h
   return best_h,best_v
```

Upsampling function:
```
def upsample_h(h,n_old,n_new):
   if n_old==n_new: return project_feasible(h.copy(),n_new)
   x_old=np.linspace(0,2,n_old); x_new=np.linspace(0,2,n_new)
   # endpoint: np.interp includes endpoint. If h at endpoints maybe okay.
   h_new=np.interp(x_new,x_old,h)
   return project_feasible(h_new,n_new)
```
Could use `np.pad`? fine.

Moving average smoothing of h:
```
def smooth_window(h, win):
   if win<=1: return h.copy()
   p=np.pad(h, win//2, mode='edge')
   c=np.cumsum(p)
   out=(c[win:] - c[:-win]) / win
   # ensure length n:
   n=len(h)
   return out[:n]
```
But pad length adds win, after subtract length n+win - win = n? Let's compute: p len n+2*win//2, c len n+pad. c[win:] length n+pad-win; c[:-win] length n+pad-win; out length n+pad-2win. If pad=2*floor(win/2), for odd win pad=win-1 => out len n-1? Simpler use `np.convolve(h, kernel, mode='same')` with normalized kernel. `mode='same'` returns n. Kernel length odd. Use small win. Top-level.
```
def smooth_window(h, win):
   if win<=1: return h.copy()
   k=np.ones(win)/win
   return np.convolve(h,k,mode='same')
```
Boundary effects okay.

def run_lbfgs_stage(h,n,L,alpha,mu,maxiter):
  bounds=[(0,1)]*n
  try:
    res=minimize(smooth_obj, h, jac=smooth_grad, args=(n,L,alpha,mu), method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={...}, callback=lbfgs_timeout_callback)
  except Exception:
    return None
  if res and res.x is not None: return project_feasible(res.x,n)

We can optimize by using slicing:
- For positive d: `prefix[n:n-0?][::-?]` maybe. Need `prefix[n-d]` for d=0..n-1 => prefix[n:0:-1] (stops before 0) length n-1 plus prefix[n] for d0? Actually prefix[n-d] for d=0..n-1: [prefix[n], prefix[n-1], ..., prefix[1]]. This is `prefix[n:0:-1]`?? Python slice prefix[n:0:-1] yields indices n,n-1,...,1 length n. (stop 0 excluded). Good.
- auto_pos: for d=0 sq, for d=1.. A=r[1:n]. So cpos = prefix[n:0:-1] - concatenate(sq, r[1:n]). Good.
- For negative d=1..n-1: total - prefix[d] - r[d]. prefix[1:n] length n-1, r[1:n]. cneg = total - prefix[1:n] - r[1:n]. Max. This avoids arange/d.
```
def compute_c_max_val(h,n,L):
    H=np.fft.rfft(h,n=L)
    r=np.fft.irfft(H*np.conjugate(H), n=L)
    prefix=np.empty(n+1); prefix[0]=0; prefix[1:]=np.cumsum(h)
    total=prefix[n]; sq=float(np.dot(h,h))
    maxv=total - sq
    if n>1:
       cpos = prefix[n:0:-1] - np.concatenate(([sq], r[1:n]))
       cneg = total - prefix[1:n] - r[1:n]
       m2=float(cpos.max()); if m2>maxv: maxv=m2
       m3=float(cneg.max()); if m3>maxv: maxv=m3
    return float(maxv)
```
This allocates cpos/cneg arrays each call; could be memory/time. We can compute max without arrays using elementwise? Need max of differences. Could use `np.subtract.reduce`? We can compute with slices in-place? For speed, allocate maybe okay. For local many calls, we can further avoid full arrays by using `np.max(cpos)`; arrays length n. Fine.

Could use `np.maximum.accumulate`? no. Keep.

Now local exact functions in detail:

`gradient_from_indices(h,n,idx,vals)`:
```
def gradient_from_indices(h,n,idx,vals):
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    center=n-1
    for a,wv in zip(idx, vals):
        k = a - center
        if k == 0:
            grad += wv * (1.0 - 2.0*h)
        elif k > 0:
            nv = n - k
            if nv > 0:
                grad[:nv] += wv * (1.0 - h[k:])
                grad[k:] -= wv * h[:nv]
        else:
            kk = -k; nv=n-kk
            if nv>0:
                grad[kk:] += wv * (1.0 - h[:nv])
                grad[:nv] -= wv * h[kk:]
    return grad
```
Make sure `h[k:]` length n-k, `h[:nv]` length nv; k=n? k max n-1, nv≥1. Good.

`select_active_indices(c,max_m, rel_tol=1e-5)`:
```
def select_active_indices(c,max_m,rel_tol=1e-5):
    vmax=float(c.max())
    thresh = vmax - max(1e-12, rel_tol*(1.0+abs(vmax)))
    active=np.where(c >= thresh)[0]
    if len(active)==0: active=np.array([len(c)//2])
    if len(active)>max_m:
        # top max_m by c
        # use argpartition for speed
        part = np.argpartition(-c[active], max_m)[:max_m]
        active = active[part]
    return active, vmax
```
If using argpartition, order not by val but top. For uniform weights order irrelevant. If weight with softmax, need maybe top sorted no matter. For gradient uniform okay.

`subgradient_improve` code:
```
def subgradient_improve(h,n,L,deadline, max_iter=100000):
    h=h.copy()
    best_h=h.copy()
    best_v=compute_c_max_val(h,n,L)
    steps=[0.04,0.02,0.01,0.005,0.0025,0.001,0.0005,0.00025,0.0001,5e-5,2.5e-5,1e-5,5e-6,2e-6]
    step_idx=0
    fail=0
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time.time() > deadline: break
       if step_idx >= len(steps): break
       step=steps[step_idx]
       c=compute_c_full(h,n,L)
       vmax=float(c.max())
       if vmax < best_v - 1e-12: best_v=vmax; best_h=h.copy()
       active, _ = select_active_indices(c, 64, 1e-6)
       vals=np.full(len(active), 1.0/len(active))
       g=gradient_from_indices(h,n,active,vals)
       g -= g.mean()
       gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
       if gmax < 1e-12: break
       d = -g / gmax
       # try sequence of step fractions? We can attempt current step and then smaller
       accepted=False
       for frac in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625]:
           h_new=np.clip(h + step*frac*d, 0.0, 1.0)
           h_new=project_feasible(h_new,n)
           v=compute_c_max_val(h_new,n,L)
           if v < best_v - 1e-12:
               h=h_new; best_v=v; best_h=h.copy(); accepted=True; break
           if time.time() > deadline: break
       if accepted:
           fail=0
       else:
           fail+=1
           if fail >= 6:
               step_idx += 1
               fail=0
       # maybe reduce step slowly if many accepts with small moves? keep
    return best_h,best_v
```
This may recompute `c` and gradient every iteration, and for each direction tries up to 5 candidate evals; expensive. With deadline 60s, maybe okay? For n=8192, each c/grad + candidate ~2-6 evals; 60s maybe 5k evals. Could be okay. But if too slow, reduce. Use `max_iter` not needed; time.
But direction with L∞ step maybe too large for small steps; candidate half etc. Good.

Potential issue: If active top M uniform gradient has zero mean? We subtract; if all g equal, gmax might be small, break. Good.

`pair_move_search` code:
```
def pair_move_search(h,n,L,deadline, top_count=128, cand_per=8, step_init=0.04):
    h=h.copy(); best_v=compute_c_max_val(h,n,L); best_h=h.copy()
    steps=[0.04,0.02,0.01,0.005,0.0025,0.001,0.0005,0.00025,0.0001,5e-5,2e-5,1e-5,5e-6,2e-6]
    failed_steps=0
    for step in steps:
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       improved_step=False
       # do multiple sweeps at same step until no improvement
       sweeps=0
       while time.time()<deadline and sweeps<5:
           sweeps+=1
           c=compute_c_full(h,n,L)
           active,_=select_active_indices(c, top_count, 1e-5)
           vals=np.full(len(active),1.0/len(active))
           g=gradient_from_indices(h,n,active,vals)
          # active gradient approximate; use all coords argsort
           order=np.argsort(g)
           low=order[:top_count]
           high=order[-top_count:][::-1]
           improved=False
           for i in low:
               if time.time()>deadline: break
               if h[i] + step > 1.0 + 1e-12: continue
               # choose candidates from high with enough value
               found=False
               for j in high:
                   if i==j or h[j] < step - 1e-12: continue
                   h[i] += step; h[j] -= step
                   v=compute_c_max_val(h,n,L)
                   if v < best_v - 1e-12:
                       best_v=v; best_h=h.copy(); improved=True; found=True; break
                   h[i]-=step; h[j]+=step
                   if cand_per and count... break? We don't track.
               if improved: break
           if not improved:
               break
           improved_step=True
       if not improved_step:
           failed_steps += 1
           if failed_steps > 8: break
       else:
           failed_steps=0
    return best_h,best_v
```
This is O(low*high cand) per sweep; high list top_count=128, cand_per perhaps 16 to cap. If we loop all high for each i, 128*128=16k candidates per sweep, too many. Need cap candidates. Use `cand_count=min(16, len(high))`. For each i, try first `cand_count` high with h[j]>=step. We can pre-filter high_for_i? Simpler loop high and count until cand_count accepted. If `h[j] < step`, continue; might skip many. Use top_count=128, cand_count=12. 128*12=1536 candidates per sweep; each eval expensive. Too many if many sweeps. Use top_count=64, cand_count=6. Then 384 candidates. If many fail, one sweep maybe 5s. Accept.
After improvement, restart sweep. Good.
But the direction based on active gradient may not be optimal; random moves might help. Add a small random component? We can use deterministic seed. Maybe not.

Maybe pair search should not recompute full FFT for each candidate? We can precompute current c and update candidate c incrementally for pair move. Updating all lags affected by pair (i,j) for each shift? For pair move, F_k changes for k=0, i-j, j-i and maybe? Because moving deltas at two coordinates affects all shifts? Let's derive: h_i +=s, h_j -=s. For a shift k, term c_k =∑ x(1-x shift). The change depends on pairs involving i or j: If k=0, change = s(1-2h_i) - s(1-2h_j) -2s^2? Actually h_i(1-h_i) change nonquadratic. For k≠0, F_k includes products h_i with h_{i+k} and h_{last} with h_i. Changing i affects shifts k = j-i? terms where i pairs with j; and linear terms for all k where i in overlap? Wait F_k has linear term ∑h_i over overlap; moving mass changes linear for many shifts, and product only shifts connecting i/j. Thus c_k changes for many k; updating all would O(n). So FFT recomputation better.

Could use FFT to update c after pair move faster? Since h changes sparse, autocorr r changes for lags k = j-i+i? Convolution of delta pair with h; O(n). Linear prefix changes O(n). Maybe we can update max without full FFT: prefix changes: adding s to h_i and -s to h_j: prefix[p] changes piecewise: for p>i +s, for p>j? Actually with -s at j. Then V_k linear part changes piecewise; A_d changes only for lags involving i or j? A_d=∑h_t h_{t+d}: change terms from pairs (i,i+d) for d such that? Changing two points, A_d changes for d= j-i or i-j? Also self for d=0 if same? For d>0, A_d includes h_i if i+d<n: term h_i h_{i+d}; and if i-d>=0: h_{i-d} h_i; similarly for j. But in F_k = linear - A_d, for given d, only if d=|i-j|? Wait A_d changes for any d where i participates: d = (i+d) offset? If change h_i, A_d includes h_i h_{i+d} for all d with i+d<n, and h_{i-d}h_i for d with i-d>=0. So many d. So updating all O(n). FFT O(nlogn) similar.

So accept full recompute.

```
def smooth_obj(x,n,L,alpha):
   h=project_feasible(x,n)
   c=compute_c_full(h,n,L)
   m=c.max(); z=alpha*(c-m); return m+logsum/alpha
def smooth_grad(x,n,L,alpha):
   h=project_feasible(x,n)
   c=compute_c_full(h,n,L); w=softmax_weights(c,alpha)
   grad=gradient_fft/h direct depending nonzero
   return grad
```

Implement `project_feasible` efficiently:
```
def project_feasible(v,n):
    h=np.clip(v,0.0,1.0)
    target=0.5*n
    s=float(np.sum(h))
    diff=target-s
    if diff>0.0:
       room=1.0-h
       tot=float(np.sum(room))
       if tot>1e-14:
          h += (diff/tot)*room
       else:
          h *= target/s if s>0 else 0.0 # shouldn't
    elif diff<0.0:
       if s>1e-14:
          h *= target/s
       else:
          h.fill(target/n) # target zero
    # final fine adjust
    return np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
```
If diff<0, scaling all by factor may make values slightly below 0? no. If some h zero, zero. Sum exactly target within float (s*target/s). Due rounding, maybe sum = target±eps. We can add final correction:
```
def fix_sum(h,n): ... # small loop using largest slack
```
Call maybe in final only; project used many times, avoid overhead. The simple sum error ~1e-15 is okay. But if scaling factor leads sum not exactly due floating, okay. For feasibility, tolerance likely. We can include final correction in force for final.

Performance of project per objective: np.clip, sum, maybe room/array operations. O(n). For n=16384 and 10k calls, 160M ops ~0.2s? fine.

def random_balanced_h(n, seed):
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    idx = rng.permutation(n)
    h = np.zeros(n)
    h[idx[:n//2]] = 1.0
    return h
```

def lbfgs_pipeline(h,n,L,deadline, stages, stage_time=45):
    h=project_feasible(h,n); best_h=h.copy(); best_v=compute_c_max_val(h,n,L)
    for alpha,mu,maxiter in stages: # mu ignored? maybe
       if time.time() > deadline - 5: break
       RUN_DEADLINE = min(deadline - 2, time.time() + stage_time)
       h_new=run_lbfgs_stage(...)
       if h_new is not None:
          v=compute_c_max_val(h_new,n,L)
          if v < best_v - 1e-13:
             best_v=v; best_h=h_new.copy()
       # if no improve, continue; maybe break if alpha large and no improve? no
    return best_h
```

Use `run_lbfgs_stage` options. Need bounds list length n; creating each stage O(n). okay.
```
def run_lbfgs_stage(h,n,L,alpha,maxiter):
    h = project_feasible(h,n)
    bounds = [(0.0,1.0)] * n
    opts={'maxiter': maxiter, 'maxfun': maxiter*8, 'ftol':1e-13, 'gtol':1e-11, 'maxcor':12, 'maxls':20}
    try:
       res = minimize(smooth_obj, h, jac=smooth_grad, args=(n,L,alpha), method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options=opts, callback=lbfgs_timeout_callback)
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res is None or not hasattr(res,'x') or res.x is None: return None
    h_new=np.asarray(res.x,float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(h_new)): return None
    return project_feasible(h_new,n)
```
Callback global `RUN_DEADLINE` set before. If timeout, `minimize` may call callback after each iteration; if raises, `res` not returned. Good.

Pseudo refined:
```
def subgradient_improve(h,n,L,deadline, top_count=64):
    h=project_feasible(h,n).copy(); best_h=h.copy(); best_v=compute_c_max_val(h,n,L)
    step_list=[0.05,0.03,0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001,0.0005,0.0002,0.0001,5e-5,2e-5,1e-5,5e-6,2e-6]
    si=0
    bad=0
    while si<len(step_list) and time.time()<deadline:
        step=step_list[si]
        improved_any=False
        attempts=0
        while attempts<25 and time.time()<deadline:
            attempts+=1
            c=compute_c_full(h,n,L)
            vmax=float(c.max())
            if vmax < best_v - 1e-12: best_v=vmax; best_h=h.copy()
            active,_=select_active_indices(c, top_count, 1e-6)
            vals=np.full(len(active),1.0/len(active))
            g=gradient_from_indices(h,n,active,vals)
            g-=g.mean()
            gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
            if gmax<1e-12: break
            d=-g/gmax
            # candidate fractions
            accepted=False
            for frac in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125):
               h_new=np.clip(h + step*frac*d,0,1)
               h_new=project_feasible(h_new,n)
               v=compute_c_max_val(h_new,n,L)
               if v<best_v-1e-12:
                   h=h_new; best_v=v; best_h=h.copy(); accepted=True; improved_any=True; break
            if accepted:
               bad=0
            else:
               bad+=1
               if bad>=3: break
        if improved_any:
           # maybe keep same step if improving; but to converge, after some improvements reduce? We can after 3 improved attempts reduce? Simpler: if accepted on multiple, still continue; but could loop. We'll reduce after a fixed number of accepted? Track count.
           # Let's reduce step every 5 accepted total:
        # outer while reuses same step until bad break; if no break due improvements forever, might spend all time at large step. We can force step reduce after e.g. 20 improvements.
```
Need prevent infinite at large step if repeatedly accepts tiny improvements but no bad. Add `improv_count`; if >20, break to next step. Good.
```
improv=0
if accepted:
   bad=0; improv+=1
   if improv>=20: break
bad_count...
```

Force sum:
```
def force_feasible(h,n):
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    target=n/2.0
    for _ in range(10):
       s=float(np.sum(h))
       diff=target-s
       if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
       if diff>0:
          idx=int(np.argmax(1.0-h))
          add=min(diff, 1.0-h[idx])
          if add<=0: break
          h[idx]+=add
       else:
          idx=int(np.argmax(h))
          sub=min(-diff, h[idx])
          if sub<=0: break
          h[idx]-=sub
    return np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
```

Let's design run step by step with code variables:
```
def run(...):
    t0=start; deadline=t0+budget-8; RUN_DEADLINE not set?
    try h_init...
    h_init=project...
    best_h=h_init; best_n=n_init; best_L=L; best_v=...; best_c=...
    # Low-up
    n_low = n_init if n_init >= 1024 else 1024
    if n_low != n_init: h_cur=upsample(h_init,n_init,n_low) else h_cur=h_init.copy()
    L_low=fft_len(n_low)
    # low stages
    if time<deadline-700:
       low_deadline=min(t0+120, deadline-700)
       h_cur=lbfgs_pipeline(h_cur,n_low,L_low,low_deadline,make_stages(n_low,'low'), stage_time=30 if n_low<=1024 else 45)
       update
    # mid 4096
    n_mid=4096
    if time<deadline-400:
       h_cur=upsample(best_h,best_n,n_mid)
       L_mid=...
       mid_deadline=min(deadline-350, time.time()+220)
       h_cur=lbfgs_pipeline(..., make_stages(n_mid,'mid'), stage_time=40)
       update
    # main 8192
    n_main=8192
    if time<deadline-160:
       h_cur=upsample(best_h,best_n,n_main)
       L_main=...
       main_deadline=min(deadline-120, time.time()+320)
       h_cur=lbfgs_pipeline(..., make_stages(n_main,'main'), stage_time=50)
       update
       # local subgrad
       if time<deadline-40:
          sub_deadline=min(deadline-30, time.time()+50)
          h_pol=subgradient_improve(h_cur,n_main,L_main,sub_deadline, top_count=96, tol_c=2e-4)
          update
       # pair
       if time<deadline-25:
          pair_deadline=min(deadline-20, time.time()+70)
          h_pol=pair_move_search(h_pol if updated else h_cur, n_main,L_main,pair_deadline, top_count=96, cand=8, step_init=0.03)
          update
    # optional high 16384
    if time<deadline-100 and best_c > 0.38070: # optional
       n_high=16384
       h_cur=upsample(best_h,best_n,n_high)
       L_high=...
       high_deadline=min(deadline-70, time.time()+250)
       h_cur=lbfgs_pipeline(..., make_stages(n_high,'high'), stage_time=60)
       update
       if time<deadline-30:
          h_pol=subgradient_improve(...30s)
          update
          h_pol=pair_move_search(...40s)
          update
    # final force and direct
    h_ret=force_feasible(best_h,best_n)
    final_c=direct_c5(h_ret,best_n)
    # if final_c > best_c + 1e-9? maybe due force; use final_c
    return h_ret, final_c, best_n
```

```
def update_best(h,n,L,best_h,best_c,best_n):
    v=compute_c_max_val(h,n,L)
    c=v*(2.0/n)
    if c < best_c - 1e-15:
        return h.copy(), c, n
    return best_h,best_c,best_n
```

`make_stages` top-level:
```
def make_stages(n, tag):
    if n <= 2048:
       per=600; alphas=[10,100,1000,10000,100000,1000000]
    elif n <= 8192:
       per=300; alphas=[10,100,1000,10000,100000,1000000]
    else:
       per=200; alphas=[10,100,500,2000,20000,100000,1000000]
    stages=[]
    for i,a in enumerate(alphas):
        mi=per
        if i >= len(alphas)-1: mi=per//2
        stages.append((a, mi))
    return stages
```

But if n=1024, per=600 and 6 stages=3600 maxiter; callback cap 30s each maybe. Good.

`fft_len`:
```
def fft_len(n):
    m = 2*n - 1
    L = 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
    return L
```
For n=1: m=1, (0).bit_length=0 ->1 <<0=1. okay. For n=500 m=999, (998).bit_length=10 ->1024. Good. For n=8192 m=16383, (16382).bit_length=14 ->16384. Good.

```
def upsample_h(h, n_old, n_new):
    h=project_feasible(h,n_old)
    if n_old==n_new: return h
    if n_new < n_old:
       xs_old=np.linspace(0,2,n_old); xs_new=np.linspace(0,2,n_new)
       hnew=np.interp(xs_new,xs_old,h); return project_feasible(hnew,n_new)
    # For upsample, linear interpolation
    xs_old=np.linspace(0,2,n_old)
    xs_new=np.linspace(0,2,n_new)
    hnew=np.interp(xs_new,xs_old,h)
    return project_feasible(hnew,n_new)
```
Could be slow for n 8192 but okay. If old n=1? not.

`smooth_obj` and `smooth_grad` use `project_feasible` which clips; bounds already. It computes c full. For value, we only need c max; for logsum need c full. okay.
```
def smooth_obj(x,n,L,alpha):
    h=project_feasible(x,n)
    c=compute_c_full(h,n,L)
    m=float(c.max())
    z=alpha*(c-m)
    return float(m + np.log(np.sum(np.exp(z)))/alpha)
```
If alpha is `np.inf`? not.
`np.sum(np.exp(z))`: z≤0; at max z=0. Underflow okay. If c max has multiple, sum≥1. If alpha large but c-m tiny, exp ~1 for active; sum maybe M. log okay.
If alpha=0? not.

`softmax_weights`:
```
def softmax_weights(c,alpha):
    m=float(c.max())
    z=alpha*(c-m)
    w=np.exp(z)
    s=w.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
       w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
    else: w /= s
    return w
```
`w` dense length 2n-1. If many underflow, okay.

Then use FFT. Good. Use threshold to avoid tiny. If alpha high but not too high, many within e^-14? for alpha 1e6, differences >1e-14? Only max. Good.

def select_active_indices(c, max_m, tol_c, n):
    vmax = float(c.max())
    scale = 0.5 * n  # because c V = C * n/2
    thresh = vmax - max(1e-12, tol_c * scale)
    active = np.where(c >= thresh)[0]
    if active.size == 0:
        active = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
    if active.size > max_m:
        # Use top max_m by value
        cact = c[active]
        if active.size > 2*max_m:
            part = np.argpartition(-cact, max_m)[:max_m]
        else:
            part = np.argsort(-cact)[:max_m]
        active = active[part]
    return active, vmax
`argpartition(-cact, max_m)` with max_m maybe equal active size? if active.size > max_m okay. But argpartition kth max_m could be out of bounds if max_m >= len(cact)? We only if >. For top max_m, kth should be max_m-1 maybe? `np.argpartition(-cact, max_m-1)[:max_m]`. If max_m=0? ensure. Use max_m at least1.
```

       update

Let's think about correctness of using `np.fft.irfft(power, n=L)` for autocorr. `power` is length L/2+1. But for autocorr, the spectrum should be complex with Hermitian symmetry. If input real to `irfft`, it assumes imaginary part zero. `|H|^2` is already the DFT of autocorr? The DFT of autocorr `r[t]=∑h_i h_{i+t}` (linear) circular with zero padding is `H * conj(H)`, which is real but not necessarily equals `|H|^2` as a real sequence of length L/2+1? It is real and even? We can pass real `power` to `irfft`; internally it constructs complex with imag=0 for positive frequencies, but expects `|H|^2` for positive frequencies. Since `|H|^2` is real and nonnegative at positive frequencies, that's correct. However the full spectrum for negative frequencies should be conjugate, which irfft handles. The zero frequency and Nyquist real. Good.

Test with real h: autocorr via `irfft(abs(rfft(h,L))**2,L)` is common. Good.

For gradient_fft convolution: `H = rfft(h); KH = rfft(K); conv=irfft(H*KH)`. Since both real signals, circular convolution. Good.

For m-i = L-d mod L, with i maybe m+d<n? It's intended as -d. Since L≥2n-1, no other wrap from positive d? For x_i with i>m? K[(m-i) mod L]=K[L-(i-m)] = K[-(i-m)] = v_{i-m} if i-m≤n-1. Good.        if time.time() > deadline - 3: break
       remaining = deadline - time.time() - 2
       if remaining < 5: break
       st_d = min(deadline - 2, time.time() + min(stage_time, remaining))
       RUN_DEADLINE = st_d  # need global in pipeline
       status,h_new=run_lbfgs_stage(...)
       if status != 'ok' or h_new is None:
          fail +=1
          if fail > 3: break
       else:
          fail=0
          v=compute_c_max_val(h_new,n,L)
          if v < best_v - 1e-12:
             best_v=v; best_h=h_new.copy()
    return project_feasible(best_h,n), best_v

def subgradient_improve(h,n,L,deadline, top_count=96, tol_c=3e-4):
    h=project_feasible(h,n).copy()
    best_h=h.copy()
    best_v=compute_c_max_val(h,n,L)
    steps=[0.05,0.03,0.02,0.012,0.007,0.004,0.002,0.001,0.0005,0.00025,0.000125,0.0000625,0.00003125]
    si=0
    while si < len(steps) and time.time() < deadline:
        step=steps[si]
        bad=0; improvements=0
        while bad < 4 and improvements < 25 and time.time() < deadline:
            c=compute_c_full(h,n,L)
            vmax=float(c.max())
            if vmax < best_v - 1e-12:
                best_v=vmax; best_h=h.copy()
            active, _ = select_active_indices(c, top_count, tol_c, n)
            vals=np.full(active.size, 1.0/active.size)
            g=gradient_from_indices(h,n,active,vals)
            g -= g.mean()
            gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
            if gmax < 1e-14:
                break
            d = -g / gmax
            accepted=False
            for frac in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625):
                h_new = h + (step*frac)*d
                np.clip(h_new,0.0,1.0,out=h_new)
                h_new = project_feasible(h_new,n)
                v=compute_c_max_val(h_new,n,L)
                if v < best_v - 1e-12:
                    h = h_new
                    best_v = v
                    best_h = h.copy()
                    accepted=True
                    improvements += 1
                    break
                if time.time() > deadline: break
            if not accepted:
                bad +=1
                # maybe try smaller step by breaking and moving to next step after bad count
        si += 1
    return project_feasible(best_h,n), best_v
```
Potential issue: If direction d has large components near bounds, `np.clip` may collapse; projection fixes sum? If clip many to 1, sum > target; projection scales down, potentially making move smaller/different. Fine.
Time: Each inner iteration computes c and gradient (FFT/direct) + up to 5 candidate FFT max. If no accept, bad count. A step may take 4*6 c eval =24 eval; with 13 steps ~312 eval. Good.
But after improving 25 times at same step, we move to next smaller step. Good.

def pair_move_search(h,n,L,deadline, top_count=96, cand_count=6, step_init=0.04, tol_c=2e-5):
    h=project_feasible(h,n).copy(); best_h=h.copy(); best_v=compute_c_max_val(h,n,L)
    steps=[step_init, step_init/2, ... down to 2e-6]
    for step in steps:
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       sweeps=0
       improved_any=False
       while sweeps<4 and time.time()<deadline:
           sweeps +=1
           c=compute_c_full(h,n.L)
           active,_=select_active_indices(c, top_count, tol_c, n)
           vals=...
           g=gradient_from_indices(...)
           order=np.argsort(g)
           low=order[:top_count]
           high=order[-top_count:][::-1]
           improved_this_sweep=False
           # Maybe reduce candidates if time. For each low:
           cnt=0
           for i in low:
              if time.time()>deadline: break
              if h[i] + step > 1.0 + 1e-12: continue
              # pick up to cand high, maybe skipping if low value insufficient

                    best_v=v; best_h=h.copy(); improved_this_sweep=True; improved_any=True

def update_best(h,n,L,best_h,best_c,best_n):
    v = compute_c_max_val(h,n,L)
    if not np.isfinite(v): return best_h,best_c,best_n
    c = v * (2.0 / n)
    if c < best_c - 1e-15:
        return h.copy(), float(c), int(n)
    return best_h,best_c,best_n
```

def random_balanced_h(n, seed):
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    h = np.zeros(int(n))
    if n >0:
       idx = rng.permutation(n)
       h[idx[:n//2]] = 1.0
    return h
```

def make_stages(n):
    if n <= 2048:
        per=700; alphas=[10,100,1000,10000,100000,1000000]
    elif n <= 8192:
        per=350; alphas=[10,100,1000,10000,100000,1000000]

    stages=[]
    for a in alphas:
       stages.append((float(a), per))
    if stages:
       stages[-1] = (stages[-1][0], max(50, per//2))
    return stages

Let's code `select_active_indices` with argpartition correctly:
```
def select_active_indices(c, max_m, tol_c, n):
    if max_m < 1: max_m=1
    vmax = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    scale = 0.5 * float(n)
    thresh = vmax - max(1e-12, float(tol_c) * scale)
    active = np.where(c >= thresh)[0]
    if active.size == 0:
        active = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
    if active.size > max_m:
        cact = c[active]
        if active.size > max_m:
            kth = max_m - 1
            if kth < 0: kth=0
            part = np.argpartition(-cact, kth)[:max_m]
        else:
            part = np.argsort(-cact)[:max_m]
        active = active[part]
    return active, vmax
```
`argpartition` with kth max_m-1 returns top max_m but not sorted. Good.

`gradient_from_indices`: If `vals` is ndarray but active maybe one; fine. Use `for i in range(active.size)`. In subgrad, vals = np.full. In pair, similar.
```
def gradient_from_indices(h,n,active,vals):
    grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    center = n-1
    for ii in range(active.size):
       a = int(active[ii]); win = float(vals[ii])
       if win == 0.0: continue
       k = a - center
       if k == 0:
          grad += win * (1.0 - 2.0*h)
       elif k > 0:
          nv = n - k
          if nv > 0:
             grad[:nv] += win * (1.0 - h[k:])
             grad[k:] -= win * h[:nv]
       else:
          kk = -k; nv = n - kk
          if nv > 0:
             grad[kk:] += win * (1.0 - h[:nv])
             grad[:nv] -= win * h[kk:]
    return grad
```
If active array unsorted, okay.

`compute_c_full` with `np.empty(2*n-1)` and slices. Let's ensure no uninitialized for n=1: c length1; guard n>1 for slices; c[0]=total-sq.
```
def compute_c_full(h,n,L):
    H = np.fft.rfft(h, n=L)
    powH = H.real*H.real + H.imag*H.imag
    r = np.fft.irfft(powH, n=L)
    prefix = np.empty(n+1, dtype=float); prefix[0]=0.0; np.cumsum(h, out=prefix[1:])
    total = float(prefix[n]); sq = float(r[0])
    c = np.empty(2*n-1, dtype=float)
    c[n-1] = total - sq
    if n > 1:
       c[:n-1] = total - prefix[n-1:0:-1] - r[n-1:0:-1]
       c[n-1:] = prefix[n:0:-1] - np.concatenate(([sq], r[1:n]))
    return c
```
`prefix[n:0:-1]` length n; okay. For n=2, prefix[2:0:-1] indices2,1 length2. r[1:n] length1. `np.concatenate(([sq], r[1:n]))` length1? Wait for n=2 positive d=0,1 length2: [sq, r[1]]. `r[1:n]` length1; plus [sq] length2. good. For n=1, no.

`compute_c_max_val`:
```
def compute_c_max_val(h,n,L):
    H=...; r=...
    prefix=...
    total=prefix[n]; sq=r[0]
    maxv=total-sq
    if n>1:
       cpos = prefix[n:0:-1] - np.concatenate(([sq], r[1:n]))
       m = float(np.max(cpos)); if m>maxv: maxv=m
       cneg = total - prefix[1:n] - r[1:n]
       m2=float(np.max(cneg)); if m2>maxv: maxv=m2
    return float(maxv)
```
Allocates cpos. Could avoid if n=1. Good.

Potential issue: `np.max(cpos)` with cpos length n maybe includes c0; okay.

`smooth_obj`:
```
def smooth_obj(x,n,L,alpha):
   h=project_feasible(x,n)
   c=compute_c_full(h,n,L)
   m=float(c.max())
   z=alpha*(c-m)
   return float(m + np.log(np.sum(np.exp(z))) / alpha)
```
If alpha very large 1e6 and c differences very small, z may be all 0 for many due floating? fine.

def smooth_grad(x,n,L,alpha):
   h=project_feasible(x,n)
   c=compute_c_full(h,n,L)
   w=softmax_weights(c,alpha)
   nz=np.count_nonzero(w > 1e-14)
   if nz <= 32:
      grad=gradient_from_indices(h,n,np.where(w>1e-14)[0],w)
   else:
      grad=gradient_fft(h,n,L,w)
   return grad
```

`gradient_fft` after direct. Let's code carefully:
```
def gradient_fft(h,n,L,w):
    center = n-1
    pos = w[center:]
    lin = np.full(n, float(pos[0]), dtype=float)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    v[0] = 2.0 * float(pos[0])
    if n > 1:
        neg = w[:center][::-1]
        v[1:] = pos[1:] + neg
        p = pos[1:]
        if p.size:
            cum = np.cumsum(p)
            totalp = float(cum[-1])
            before = np.empty(p.size, dtype=float)
            before[0] = 0.0
            before[1:] = cum[:-1]
            lin[:-1] += totalp - before
            if neg.size:
                cumn = np.cumsum(neg)
                lin[1:] += cumn
        else:
            pass
    K = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    K[0] = v[0]
    if n > 1:
        K[1:n] = v[1:]
        K[L-(n-1):] = v[1:][::-1]
    H = np.fft.rfft(h, n=L)
    KH = np.fft.rfft(K, n=L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(H * KH, n=L)
    return lin - conv[:n]
```
Check `lin[:-1] += totalp - before`: p size n-1. For j=0..n-2, before[j] = sum p[0..j-1]? before[0]=0, before[1]=p0, before[j]=sum p0..p_{j-1}. totalp - before[j] = sum p[j..] i.e. d≥j+1. For j=0 totalp (d1..n-1). For j=n-2 totalp - sum p0..n-3 = p[n-2] (d=n-1). Correct. `lin[n-1]` no positive. Good.
`lin[1:] += cumn`: cumn[j-1]=sum neg[0..j-1], d=1..j. correct.
`K[L-(n-1):] = v[1:][::-1]` length n-1? Slice from L-n+1 to end length n-1. For n=2, L-(1)=L-1, assign K[L-1]=v1. good.

But note for n>1, `neg = w[:center][::-1]` length center=n-1. `v[1:] = pos[1:] + neg`; pos[1:] length n-1. Good.

Potential bug: If weights sum not exactly 1 due threshold? `w` softmax sum exact. If direct threshold in smooth_grad for sparse, uses w values (sum 1). Good.

```
def run_lbfgs_stage(h,n,L,alpha,maxiter):
    h = project_feasible(h,n)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    opts = {'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'maxfun': int(int(maxiter)*8+10), 'ftol':1e-14, 'gtol':1e-10, 'maxcor':20, 'maxls':20}
    try:
       res = minimize(smooth_obj, h, jac=smooth_grad, args=(n,L,float(alpha)), method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options=opts, callback=lbfgs_timeout_callback)
    except RuntimeError:
       return 'timeout', None
    except Exception:
       return 'error', None
    if res is None or res.x is None: return 'error', None
    hnew = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(hnew)): return 'error', None
    return 'ok', project_feasible(hnew,n)
```

    best_h = h.copy()

       if time.time() > deadline - 3: break

       st_time = min(stage_time, rem)
       if st_time < 5: break
       RUN_DEADLINE = time.time() + st_time

```

```python
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import minimize

RUN_DEADLINE = 0.0


def lbfgs_timeout_callback(*args):
    if RUN_DEADLINE > 0.0 and time.time() > RUN_DEADLINE:
        raise RuntimeError("budget")


def fft_len(n):
    m = 2 * int(n) - 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def project_feasible(v, n):
    h = np.asarray(v, dtype=float)
    h = h.copy()
    h[~np.isfinite(h)] = 0.5
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    n = int(n)
    target = 0.5 * n
    s = float(np.sum(h))
    diff = target - s
    if diff > 0.0:
        room = 1.0 - h
        tot = float(np.sum(room))
        if tot > 1e-14:
            h += (diff / tot) * room
        else:
            h.fill(0.5)
    elif diff < 0.0:
        if s > 1e-14:
            h *= (target / s)
        else:
            h.fill(0.5)
    return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)


def upsample_h(h, n_old, n_new):
    h = project_feasible(h, n_old)
    n_old = int(n_old)
    n_new = int(n_new)
    if n_old == n_new:
        return h
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_old)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_new)
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, h)
    return project_feasible(out, n_new)


def compute_c_full(h, n, L):
    H = np.fft.rfft(h, n=L)
    power = H.real * H.real + H.imag * H.imag
    r = np.fft.irfft(power, n=L)

    prefix = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=float)
    prefix[0] = 0.0
    np.cumsum(h, out=prefix[1:])
    total = float(prefix[n])
    sq = float(r[0])

    c = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    c[n - 1] = total - sq
    if n > 1:
        c[:n - 1] = total - prefix[n - 1:0:-1] - r[n - 1:0:-1]
        c[n - 1:] = prefix[n:0:-1] - np.concatenate(([sq], r[1:n]))
    return c


def compute_c_max_val(h, n, L):
    H = np.fft.rfft(h, n=L)
    power = H.real * H.real + H.imag * H.imag
    r = np.fft.irfft(power, n=L)

    prefix = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=float)
    prefix[0] = 0.0
    np.cumsum(h, out=prefix[1:])
    total = float(prefix[n])
    sq = float(r[0])

    maxv = total - sq
    if n > 1:
        cpos = prefix[n:0:-1] - np.concatenate(([sq], r[1:n]))
        m1 = float(np.max(cpos))
        if m1 > maxv:
            maxv = m1
        cneg = total - prefix[1:n] - r[1:n]
        m2 = float(np.max(cneg))
        if m2 > maxv:
            maxv = m2
    return float(maxv)


def softmax_weights(c, alpha):
    m = float(np.max(c))
    z = float(alpha) * (c - m)
    w = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros(len(c), dtype=float)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= s
    return w


def gradient_fft(h, n, L, w):
    center = n - 1
    pos = w[center:]
    lin = np.full(n, float(pos[0]), dtype=float)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    v[0] = 2.0 * float(pos[0])

    if n > 1:
        neg = w[:center][::-1]
        v[1:] = pos[1:] + neg

        p = pos[1:]
        if p.size:
            cum = np.cumsum(p)
            totalp = float(cum[-1])
            before = np.empty(p.size, dtype=float)
            before[0] = 0.0
            before[1:] = cum[:-1]
            lin[:-1] += totalp - before

        if neg.size:
            cumn = np.cumsum(neg)
            lin[1:] += cumn

    K = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    K[0] = v[0]
    if n > 1:
        K[1:n] = v[1:]
        K[L - (n - 1):] = v[1:][::-1]

    H = np.fft.rfft(h, n=L)
    KH = np.fft.rfft(K, n=L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(H * KH, n=L)
    return lin - conv[:n]


def gradient_from_indices(h, n, active, vals):
    grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    center = n - 1
    for ii in range(int(active.size)):
        a = int(active[ii])
        win = float(vals[ii])
        if win == 0.0:
            continue
        k = a - center
        if k == 0:
            grad += win * (1.0 - 2.0 * h)
        elif k > 0:
            nv = n - k
            if nv > 0:
                grad[:nv] += win * (1.0 - h[k:])
                grad[k:] -= win * h[:nv]
        else:
            kk = -k
            nv = n - kk
            if nv > 0:
                grad[kk:] += win * (1.0 - h[:nv])
                grad[:nv] -= win * h[kk:]
    return grad


def select_active_indices(c, max_m, tol_c, n):
    max_m = max(1, int(max_m))
    vmax = float(np.max(c))
    scale = 0.5 * float(n)
    thresh = vmax - max(1e-12, float(tol_c) * scale)
    active = np.where(c >= thresh)[0]
    if active.size == 0:
        active = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
    if active.size > max_m:
        cact = c[active]
        kth = max(0, max_m - 1)
        part = np.argpartition(-cact, kth)[:max_m]
        active = active[part]
    return active, vmax


def smooth_obj(x, n, L, alpha):
    h = project_feasible(x, n)
    c = compute_c_full(h, n, L)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    z = float(alpha) * (c - m)
    return float(m + np.log(float(np.sum(np.exp(z)))) / float(alpha))


def smooth_grad(x, n, L, alpha):
    h = project_feasible(x, n)
    c = compute_c_full(h, n, L)
    w = softmax_weights(c, alpha)
    nz = np.count_nonzero(w > 1e-14)
    if nz <= 32:
        idx = np.where(w > 1e-14)[0]
        return gradient_from_indices(h, n, idx, w)
    return gradient_fft(h, n, L, w)


def run_lbfgs_stage(h, n, L, alpha, maxiter):
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * int(n)
    mi = max(1, int(maxiter))
    opts = {
        "maxiter": mi,
        "maxfun": mi * 8 + 10,
        "ftol": 1e-14,
        "gtol": 1e-10,
        "maxcor": 20,
        "maxls": 20,
    }
    try:
        res = minimize(
            smooth_obj,
            h,
            jac=smooth_grad,
            args=(n, L, float(alpha)),
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            bounds=bounds,
            options=opts,
            callback=lbfgs_timeout_callback,
        )
    except RuntimeError:
        return "timeout", None
    except Exception:
        return "error", None
    if res is None or res.x is None:
        return "error", None
    hnew = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(hnew)):
        return "error", None
    return "ok", project_feasible(hnew, n)


def lbfgs_pipeline(h, n, L, deadline, stages, stage_time=45.0):
    global RUN_DEADLINE
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_v = compute_c_max_val(h, n, L)
    fail = 0

    for alpha, maxiter in stages:
        if time.time() > deadline - 3.0:
            break
        rem = deadline - time.time() - 2.0
        if rem < 5.0:
            break
        st_time = min(float(stage_time), rem)
        if st_time < 5.0:
            break
        RUN_DEADLINE = time.time() + st_time
        status, hnew = run_lbfgs_stage(best_h, n, L, alpha, maxiter)
        if status == "ok" and hnew is not None:
            v = compute_c_max_val(hnew, n, L)
            if np.isfinite(v) and v < best_v - 1e-12:
                best_v = float(v)
                best_h = hnew.copy()
            fail = 0
        else:
            fail += 1
            if fail > 3:
                break

    return project_feasible(best_h, n)


def make_stages(n):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 2048:
        per = 700
        alphas = [10.0, 100.0, 1000.0, 10000.0, 100000.0, 1000000.0]
    elif n <= 8192:
        per = 350
        alphas = [10.0, 100.0, 1000.0, 10000.0, 100000.0, 1000000.0]
    else:
        per = 220
        alphas = [10.0, 50.0, 200.0, 1000.0, 5000.0, 50000.0, 200000.0, 2000000.0]

    stages = []
    for a in alphas:
        stages.append((float(a), per))
    if stages:
        stages[-1] = (stages[-1][0], max(50, int(per) // 2))
    return stages


def random_balanced_h(n, seed):
    n = int(n)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(int(seed))
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if n == 0:
        return h
    idx = rng.permutation(n)
    k = n // 2
    if k > 0:
        h[idx[:k]] = 1.0
    if n % 2 == 1:
        h[idx[k]] = 0.5
    return h


def subgradient_improve(h, n, L, deadline, top_count=96, tol_c=3e-4):
    h = project_feasible(h, n).copy()
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_v = compute_c_max_val(h, n, L)

    steps = [
        0.05, 0.03, 0.02, 0.012, 0.007, 0.004,
        0.002, 0.001, 0.0005, 0.00025, 0.000125, 0.0000625, 0.00003125
    ]

    si = 0
    while si < len(steps) and time.time() < deadline:
        step = steps[si]
        bad = 0
        improvements = 0

        while bad < 4 and improvements < 25 and time.time() < deadline:
            c = compute_c_full(h, n, L)
            vmax = float(np.max(c))
            if not np.isfinite(vmax):
                return best_h, best_v
            if vmax < best_v - 1e-12:
                best_v = vmax
                best_h = h.copy()

            active, _ = select_active_indices(c, int(top_count), tol_c, n)
            vals = np.full(active.size, 1.0 / float(active.size), dtype=float)
            g = gradient_from_indices(h, n, active, vals)
            g -= float(np.mean(g))
            gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
            if gmax < 1e-14:
                return project_feasible(best_h, n), best_v

            d = -g / gmax
            accepted = False
            for frac in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625):
                h_new = h + (step * frac) * d
                np.clip(h_new, 0.0, 1.0, out=h_new)
                h_new = project_feasible(h_new, n)
                v = compute_c_max_val(h_new, n, L)
                if np.isfinite(v) and v < best_v - 1e-12:
                    h = h_new
                    best_v = v
                    best_h = h.copy()
                    accepted = True
                    improvements += 1
                    break
                if time.time() > deadline:
                    break
            if not accepted:
                bad += 1
        si += 1

    return project_feasible(best_h, n), best_v


def pair_move_search(h, n, L, deadline, top_count=64, cand_count=4, step_init=0.04, tol_c=2e-5):
    h = project_feasible(h, n).copy()
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_v = compute_c_max_val(h, n, L)

    steps = []
    s = float(step_init)
    while s > 1e-7:
        steps.append(s)
        s *= 0.5
    if len(steps) < 12:
        steps.append(5e-6)
        steps.append(2e-6)

    top_count = max(8, int(top_count))
    cand_count = max(2, int(cand_count))
    no_improve_steps = 0

    for step in steps:
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        improved_any = False
        sweeps = 0
        max_sweeps = 4

        while sweeps < max_sweeps and time.time() < deadline:
            sweeps += 1
            c = compute_c_full(h, n, L)
            active, _ = select_active_indices(c, top_count, tol_c, n)
            vals = np.full(active.size, 1.0 / float(active.size), dtype=float)
            g = gradient_from_indices(h, n, active, vals)

            order = np.argsort(g)
            low = order[:top_count]
            high = order[-top_count:][::-1]

            improved_sweep = False
            total_tried = 0
            max_tried = top_count * cand_count

            for i in low:
                if total_tried >= max_tried:
                    break
                i = int(i)
                if h[i] + step > 1.0 + 1e-12:
                    continue
                jtried = 0
                for j in high:
                    if total_tried >= max_tried:
                        break
                    j = int(j)
                    if j == i:
                        continue
                    if h[j] < step - 1e-12:
                        continue
                    jtried += 1
                    total_tried += 1
                    old_i = float(h[i])
                    old_j = float(h[j])
                    h[i] = old_i + step
                    h[j] = old_j - step
                    v = compute_c_max_val(h, n, L)
                    if np.isfinite(v) and v < best_v - 1e-12:
                        best_v = v
                        best_h = h.copy()
                        improved_sweep = True
                        break
                    h[i] = old_i
                    h[j] = old_j
                    if jtried >= cand_count:
                        break
                if improved_sweep:
                    break

            if not improved_sweep:
                break
            improved_any = True

        if not improved_any:
            no_improve_steps += 1
            if no_improve_steps > 5:
                break
        else:
            no_improve_steps = 0

    return project_feasible(best_h, n), best_v


def update_best(h, n, L, best_h, best_c, best_n):
    v = compute_c_max_val(h, n, L)
    if not np.isfinite(v):
        return best_h, best_c, best_n
    c = v * (2.0 / float(n))
    if c < best_c - 1e-15:
        return h.copy(), float(c), int(n)
    return best_h, best_c, best_n


def force_feasible(h, n):
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    target = 0.5 * int(n)
    for _ in range(12):
        s = float(np.sum(h))
        diff = target - s
        if abs(diff) < 1e-14:
            break
        if diff > 0.0:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
            add = min(diff, 1.0 - h[idx])
            if add <= 0.0:
                break
            h[idx] += add
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
            sub = min(-diff, h[idx])
            if sub <= 0.0:
                break
            h[idx] -= sub
    return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)


def direct_c5(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = int(n)
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")) * (2.0 / n))


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + max(1.0, float(budget_s) - 25.0)
    np.random.seed(int(seed))

    try:
        h_init = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n_init = int(len(h_init))
    except Exception:
        n_init = 1024
        h_init = random_balanced_h(n_init, seed)

    if n_init <= 0:
        n_init = 1024
        h_init = random_balanced_h(n_init, seed)

    h_init = project_feasible(h_init, n_init)
    L_init = fft_len(n_init)
    best_h = h_init.copy()
    best_v = compute_c_max_val(best_h, n_init, L_init)
    best_c = best_v * (2.0 / float(n_init)) if np.isfinite(best_v) else 1.0
    best_n = n_init

    # Low-resolution warm-up.
    n_low = max(1024, n_init)
    if n_low != n_init:
        h_work = upsample_h(h_init, n_init, n_low)
    else:
        h_work = h_init.copy()
    L_low = fft_len(n_low)

    if time.time() < deadline - 800.0:
        low_deadline = min(t0 + 120.0, deadline - 800.0)
        if low_deadline > time.time() + 15.0:
            h_work = lbfgs_pipeline(
                h_work, n_low, L_low, low_deadline,
                make_stages(n_low), stage_time=30.0
            )
            best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h_work, n_low, L_low, best_h, best_c, best_n)

    # Mid-resolution improvement.
    n_mid = 4096
    if time.time() < deadline - 450.0:
        h_work = upsample_h(best_h, best_n, n_mid)
        L_mid = fft_len(n_mid)
        mid_deadline = min(time.time() + 240.0, deadline - 380.0)
        if mid_deadline > time.time() + 20.0:
            h_work = lbfgs_pipeline(
                h_work, n_mid, L_mid, mid_deadline,
                make_stages(n_mid), stage_time=45.0
            )
            best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h_work, n_mid, L_mid, best_h, best_c, best_n)

    # Main high-resolution optimization.
    n_main = 8192
    if time.time() < deadline - 220.0:
        h_work = upsample_h(best_h, best_n, n_main)
        L_main = fft_len(n_main)
        main_deadline = min(time.time() + 320.0, deadline - 130.0)
        if main_deadline > time.time() + 25.0:
            h_work = lbfgs_pipeline(
                h_work, n_main, L_main, main_deadline,
                make_stages(n_main), stage_time=50.0
            )
        best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h_work, n_main, L_main, best_h, best_c, best_n)

        if time.time() < deadline - 70.0:
            sub_deadline = min(time.time() + 50.0, deadline - 50.0)
            if sub_deadline > time.time() + 8.0:
                h_work, _ = subgradient_improve(
                    h_work, n_main, L_main, sub_deadline,
                    top_count=96, tol_c=3e-4
                )
                best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h_work, n_main, L_main, best_h, best_c, best_n)

        if time.time() < deadline - 35.0:
            pair_deadline = min(time.time() + 60.0, deadline - 25.0)
            if pair_deadline > time.time() + 8.0:
                h_work, _ = pair_move_search(
                    h_work, n_main, L_main, pair_deadline,
                    top_count=64, cand_count=4, step_init=0.04, tol_c=2e-5
                )
                best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h_work, n_main, L_main, best_h, best_c, best_n)

    # Optional extra-high-resolution attempt if there is substantial time left.
    if time.time() < deadline - 130.0 and best_c > 0.38070:
        n_high = 16384
        h_work = upsample_h(best_h, best_n, n_high)
        L_high = fft_len(n_high)
        high_deadline = min(time.time() + 260.0, deadline - 90.0)
        if high_deadline > time.time() + 25.0:
            h_work = lbfgs_pipeline(
                h_work, n_high, L_high, high_deadline,
                make_stages(n_high), stage_time=60.0
            )
            best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h_work, n_high, L_high, best_h, best_c, best_n)

            if time.time() < deadline - 55.0:
                sub_deadline = min(time.time() + 40.0, deadline - 40.0)
                if sub_deadline > time.time() + 8.0:
                    h_work, _ = subgradient_improve(
                        h_work, n_high, L_high, sub_deadline,
                        top_count=128, tol_c=3e-4
                    )
                    best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h_work, n_high, L_high, best_h, best_c, best_n)

            if time.time() < deadline - 30.0:
                pair_deadline = min(time.time() + 40.0, deadline - 20.0)
                if pair_deadline > time.time() + 8.0:
                    h_work, _ = pair_move_search(
                        h_work, n_high, L_high, pair_deadline,
                        top_count=80, cand_count=3, step_init=0.03, tol_c=2e-5
                    )
                    best_h, best_c, best_n = update_best(h_work, n_high, L_high, best_h, best_c, best_n)

    # Final exact evaluation and feasibility repair.
    final_h = force_feasible(best_h, best_n)
    final_c = direct_c5(final_h, best_n)
    if not np.isfinite(final_c):
        final_c = best_c

    return final_h, final_c, int(best_n)
```
